# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

 succeeded in 773ms:
---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Independent relation-map review — round 19

Reviewed **`c476d9d559ce5e723c6f5d91fad5e6c3599cfe93`**. **CHANGES REQUIRED: two P1 originator-refusal failures.** `radio-labelled trastuzumab` and `trastuzumab labelled with 99m-technetium` pass every refusal stage and draw unlabelled TRASTUZUMAB. Both show 13 map trials; trial/sponsor QA each returns 12 originator evidence rows. Their joined-label / element-first controls refuse. Both holes reproduce in HEAD^; they are not claimed as originator regressions introduced by this commit.

Exact prior promoted M1–M18 primary name findings are FIXED, including M18-B1 `trastuzumab radiolabelled with iodine-124`. Historical supplemental gaps and accepted exceptions retain individual NOT FIXED dispositions below. The user-listed over-refusals and ChEMBL bevacizumab-code limitation do not determine the verdict. New `non-radiolabelled trastuzumab` over-refusal is separately reported as a should-fix.

No production code or tracked file was edited. New scratch files are confined to `audit_evidence_claims/m19/`.

## Method, evidence, and limits

The review tests four claims separately: prior exact failures stay repaired; equivalent tracer descriptions never select an unlabelled molecule; displayed edges/values agree with live stored rows; request cost remains bounded. Paired inputs change only the relevant spelling, direct helper traces identify the missed guard, and independently owned original CLI servers establish the public response. A passing test suite alone is not treated as proof of the refusal guarantee.

## M19-B1 — P1: hyphenated radio-labelling words still draw the originator

Exact input: **`radio-labelled trastuzumab`**. `POST /api/explore` returns HTTP 200 with **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, **13 trials**, and no refusal. `Which trials evaluate radio-labelled trastuzumab?` and `Who sponsors trials of radio-labelled trastuzumab?` each return **12 originator evidence rows**. Removing only the hyphen, producing `radiolabelled trastuzumab`, returns a refusal and zero QA evidence.

This is an ordinary spelling of a tracer description, not a contrived isotope spelling: a primary experimental article develops and evaluates isotope-labelled trastuzumab and uses the exact phrase “radio-labelled trastuzumab” in its Discussion. [Lub-de Hooge et al., Preclinical characterisation of 111In-DTPA-trastuzumab](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC1575276/). The article supports the terminology and tracer/originator distinction; it does not supply graph evidence or a clinical recommendation.

| Stage | `radio-labelled trastuzumab` | `radiolabelled trastuzumab` |
|---|---|---|
| Intervention resolver | CONTAINED_ALIAS + UNRESOLVED, no BLOCKED | CONTAINED_ALIAS + UNRESOLVED, no BLOCKED |
| `_resolver_blocked` | `{}` | `{}` |
| `_qualified` | false | false |
| `_item_has_isotope` | false | true |
| Actual `_marked` call | `None`; both tokens in segment 0 | refusal |
| `_whole_name` | `None` | `None` |
| Public linker | Drug TRASTUZUMAB, no Refused | Refused only |
| HTTP map | CHEMBL1201585, 13 trials | refused, no centre |
| Trial / sponsor QA | 12 / 12 | 0 / 0 |

**Cause:** `src/query.py:177-179` splits the phrase into alphanumeric words and tests each word independently for membership in `_LABEL_WORDS` or a `radiolabel`/`radioiod` prefix. The hyphenated word becomes `radio`, `labelled`; neither passes. The earlier patterns in `src/textrules.py:306-318` recognise isotope-symbol syntax, not this word. The resolver records no BLOCKED component (`src/query.py:303-343`), `_qualified` misses it (`src/query.py:232-256`), and the item-wide check in `_marked` is false (`src/query.py:423-425`). `Engine.explore` selects the surviving positive hit (`src/app.py:88-95`). The new test only checks joined `radiolabelled` (`tests/test_app.py:428-440`).

**Corroborating exact inputs:** `radio-labeled trastuzumab`, `radio labelled trastuzumab`, `radio‑labelled trastuzumab` (U+2011), `radio_labelled trastuzumab`, `RADIO-LABELLED TRASTUZUMAB`, `trastuzumab (radio-labelled)`, `trastuzumab radio-iodinated`, `trastuzumab radio-tracer`. These all draw the originator, while their joined control spellings refuse. `radio-labelled Herceptin`, `radio-labelled vinblastine`, and `radio-labelled erlotinib` show the same guard failure across aliases/drugs. They are common-cause software probes; no claim is made that every synthetic descriptor denotes a marketed product.

The same new lexical check is incomplete for ordinary morphology: `trastuzumab radioactively labelled`, `radioconjugated trastuzumab`, `trastuzumab radioimmunoconjugates`, and `trastuzumab radiopharmaceuticals` survive, while `trastuzumab radioactive label` and `trastuzumab radiopharmaceutical` refuse. These are corroborations of incomplete labelling-word recognition, not separate counted blockers.

## M19-B2 — P1: mass-first metastable full element name bypasses the reverse-order rule

Exact input: **`trastuzumab labelled with 99m-technetium`**. The HTTP map again selects CHEMBL1201585 with 13 trials and no refusal; trial and sponsor QA each return 12 originator evidence rows. Change only the isotope spelling to `technetium-99m` or `99mTc`: the map refuses and both QA evidence arrays are empty. `99m-technetium trastuzumab`, `99m technetium trastuzumab`, and the fused `technetium99m trastuzumab` also survive. The direct paired case is the explicit labelled product, not a drug merely mentioned alongside a separate isotope.

Tc-99m-labelled trastuzumab is an actual radiopharmaceutical research product: the original RP-39 abstract reports its preparation via DTPA for imaging. [Bhusari et al., Radiopharmacy RP-39](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3868035/). The equivalence of the tested isotope notations is the reviewer's inference from that identity and the application's own accepted/refused controls.

**Cause:** the general two-order element-name loop requires `num.isdigit()` (`src/query.py:181-185`), so `99m` is rejected as a mass. Its special exception checks only `a == "technetium" and b in ("99m", "99")` (`src/query.py:186-187`); the reversed order is never tested. The symbol guards do not convert the full element name. `_qualified == false`, `_resolver_blocked == {}`, `_item_has_isotope == false`, actual `_marked == None`, `_whole_name == None`, and the public linker has a positive Drug and no Refused. Thus this is a second concrete failure mechanism within the new helper.

**Required behaviour for both blockers:** recognise equivalent explicit labelling descriptions consistently with their refused controls; no unlabelled TRASTUZUMAB map or originator QA evidence may survive. No application repair was made.

## Should-fix and additional observations

- **M19-S1, new over-refusal:** `non-radiolabelled trastuzumab` now refuses, whereas HEAD^ and current `unlabelled trastuzumab` draw TRASTUZUMAB. Splitting the `non-` prefix leaves a matching label word, without negation handling. This is a new recall regression, not an originator-leak blocker, and is distinct from the user's previously accepted over-refusals.
- Full element names fused to mass remain unrecognised in several ordinary compact renderings: `iodine124 trastuzumab`, `trastuzumab iodine124`, `zirconium89 trastuzumab`, `89zirconium trastuzumab`. Their hyphenated controls refuse. Recorded as supplements; B1/B2 suffice for rejection.
- `tritiated FDG` and `FDG labelled with carbon-14` select the already-labelled F18 preferred product despite `_item_has_isotope == true`. This extends the previous isotope-compatibility concern in m18; no independent product-identity proof is supplied here, so it is not promoted to another blocker.
- Ordinary controls `trastuzumab`, `osimertinib 80 mg`, `paclitaxel 2 cycles`, and `lead compound osimertinib` remain available. The intended labelled-product control `fluorine-18 fluorodeoxyglucose` still selects FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18. Broader demo and cost checks belong to the other reviewers.

### M16-S1: the item's isotope state is now computed once

**FIXED for the exact reported extra work.** At 125 / 1,000 occurrences, instrumentation records **one whole-item scan** and **250 / 2,000 within-word helper calls**, respectively. M16 originally recorded 125 / 1,000 whole-item scans and 15,625 / 1,000,000 within-word calls. M17 recorded one scan with 125 / 1,000 within-word calls. The preceding M18 repair checks both the canonical word and its punctuation piece, which are identical for `isa`; this doubles those within-word calls but does not restore per-match whole-item rescanning. `@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=4096)` caches the pure tuple-keyed computation (`src/query.py:168-198`), called from `_marked` at `src/query.py:423-425`. The same single ISATUXIMAB entity survives. [Fresh instrumentation](cost/repeated_match_instrumentation.json), [reused script](cost/repeated_match_followup.py).

For the legal 3,999-character **answer** field, the fresh HTTP check takes **2.44896 s**, queues EGFR for **2.36627 s**, and takes **2.95730 s** warm. The ordinary map after completion takes 0.04612 s; all return 200. This verifies the repair without claiming all repeated-match work was removed. `_marked` still constructs/scans token lists before checking the cache and runs before entity deduplication (`src/query.py:368-396,581-591`). [HTTP](cost/isolated_repeated_match_cost_http.json), [closed owned server / unchanged DB](cost/repeated_match_cost_lifecycle.json).

`engine.lock` serializes API operations (`src/app.py:247-263`). These 3,999/4,000-character workloads use direct linking or the **answer field**; explore correctly rejects more than 500 characters. Limits precede NFKC (`src/app.py:239-243,260`; `src/query.py:117-118,282-284`). Residual costs are should-fix observations, not the reason for the verdict. **No model calls or model cost were incurred.**

## Specification


Specification source: the user's Round 19 brief and original guarantee 3 / approval bar: “a refused product drawn as the originator” is a blocker. Diff reviewed: `git diff 4bed79c1f170ebe1efa3d452ce4398cce17b2113...HEAD -- src tests`.

**Two P1 failures.** (1) `radio-labelled trastuzumab` is a published ordinary tracer description, but every guard misses the separated label word (`src/query.py:177-179`); actual HTTP draws CHEMBL1201585 with 13 trials, and trial/sponsor questions each return 12 originator evidence rows. The joined `radiolabelled` spelling refuses. (2) `trastuzumab labelled with 99m-technetium` produces the same wrong map and evidence; reversing the isotope to `technetium-99m` refuses. The full-element loop rejects `99m` as nonnumeric and handles the special metastable form in only one order (`src/query.py:181-187`).

The exact repair examples are fixed. Both new holes reproduce on HEAD^ with current data/app modules; neither is claimed as a newly introduced originator regression. New `non-radiolabelled trastuzumab` refusal is a separate should-fix recall regression. No scope-creep finding is identified. Known over-refusals and bevacizumab biosimilar-code curation do not determine these findings.

Specification: **2 P1 findings; CHANGES REQUIRED.**

Tests 325.

## Round 4 (audit_evidence_claims/m4) — CHANGES REQUIRED

Shortest-span binding kept failing (wrong drug on ties, a six-token reach, qualifiers already consumed by the token pass).
Replaced by a fail-closed rule: text is cut into list items at , ; ， ； 、 and listing words; an item in which any run of
words names a distinct product or tracer has every drug in it refused (which drug the qualifier describes is not guessed);
drugs in other items, and targets/companies in the same item, still link. M4-B1 (both drugs now refused, never
BEVACIZUMAB), M4-B2 (no distance cap), M4-B3 (one pass, no pre-consumed qualifier) and the EGFR/AstraZeneca over-refusal
are covered by tests. Tests 326.

## Round 5 (audit_evidence_claims/m5) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M5-B1 | a glued "/" or "|" ("bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib") hid the suffix guard; the alias key dropped the slash and linked BEVACIZUMAB | tokens are stripped of "/" and "|", which end the list item |
| M5-B2 | "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": "and" detached the bare qualifier | a qualifier item with no drug carries across a listing word to the next item's drugs; a product named by itself (in the index, or a known biosimilar/reformulation brand such as Mvasi, Abraxane) does not carry |
| M5-S1 | names were built across list items ("biosimilar anastrozole, Arimidex" lost Arimidex; "Erlotinib, hydrochloride ..." became the salt) | drug spans and the positive matcher stay inside one item |
| M5-S3 | the all-runs scan was cubic (1.8 s at 250 words, under the lock) | whole item once for unanchored patterns, runs of at most eight words for anchored ones: 500 words in about 0.03 s |
| M5-S2 | "Pegylated interferon alfa-2a" (a stored alias of the pegylated drug itself) is refused | not changed: the resolver refuses it too, and exempting known aliases from the product guards would reopen M5-B1 (recorded as a known over-refusal) |

Tests 329.

## Round 6 (audit_evidence_claims/m6) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M6-B1 | an interior "/" or "|" ("bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib") hid the suffix | "/" and "|" split a word wherever they are and end the list item |
| M6-B2 | edge punctuation the guards kept but the alias key dropped ("bevacizumab-awwb。", trailing hyphen) | edge punctuation is stripped before the guards (brackets kept for isotopes) |
| M6-B3 | "and" inside a parenthetical description detached its qualifier from the preceding drug | listing words inside parentheses do not split |
| all | the linker kept re-implementing the resolver's refusals | the intervention resolver now runs on each list piece; a drug inside a piece it refuses as a distinct product or tracer is never linked (unless the same molecule is also accepted in another piece, which the list-item rule then governs) |
| M6-S1 | complete stored names with list punctuation picked another drug | an input that is exactly one stored name (and not refused) is read whole |

Tests 331.

## Round 7 (audit_evidence_claims/m7) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M7-B1 | the whole-name shortcut ran before the list linker and overrode its refusal ("bevacizumab-awwb?") | the shortcut runs last and only if neither the resolver nor the list linker refused anything |
| M7-B2 | wrappers and Unicode hyphens ("(bevacizumab-awwb)", U+2011) defeated both guards | input is NFKC-normalised and every dash folded to "-" before any check; all edge punctuation and wrappers are stripped from words |
| M7-B3 | "89Zr trastuzumab" (space-separated isotope) passed both guards | an isotope label anywhere in a list item qualifies the item |
| M7-S1 | the whole-name shortcut used the aggressive key ("is a", "Erlotinib, hydrochloride") | only a letter-for-letter stored name (after the same normalisation) is read whole |
| M7-S2 | exact stored names with "/" or a listing word were rejected first | the exact-name check no longer pre-rejects them |
| M7-S3 | the resolver ran on every piece | each distinct piece once, only if it holds a drug name, with a per-engine cache |

Tests 334.

## Round 8 (audit_evidence_claims/m8) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M8-B1 | "[89 Zr] trastuzumab": the resolver's tracer refusal was re-tested on its cleaned component and lost | the resolver's `radiotracer_guard` rule is honoured as recorded; an isotope label written as two words ("89 Zr") beside a name qualifies the item |
| M8-B2 | "bevacizumab-(awwb)": interior wrappers defeated the anchored guard while the alias key ignored them | the guards also see each item with every run of punctuation (not spaces) read as one hyphen |
| M8-B3 | dashes outside the hand-written list (U+2E3A, 14 of 26 Pd) | every Pd character and the minus signs fold to "-"; a test covers all 26 |
| M8-S1 | cache rollover cleared the entry it was about to read (KeyError, 400) | the result is kept in a local before the cache is touched |
| M8-S2 | a bare qualifier carried across a comma ("biosimilar, and osimertinib") | carry only within the same comma/semicolon piece |
| M8-S3 | lone stored names shaped like isotopes ("566C", "9-ac", "5ac") refused | a lone stored name in its own piece is not refused by the tracer rule; an isotope qualifies only beside other words |
| M8-S4 | a stored name's final period stripped before comparison | both sides are stripped alike |

Tests 336.

## Round 9 (audit_evidence_claims/m9) — CHANGES REQUIRED

The positive lookup key (letters and digits only) was broader than every refusal check. Refusal now runs on that same form:

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M9-B1 | "bevacizumab_awwb", "bevácizumab-awwb" | item guards also see the text accent-stripped and with underscores and punctuation folded; a matched name whose key ends in a known biosimilar suffix (stem = the same drug) is refused |
| M9-B2 | "[89.Zr] trastuzumab", "89:Zr trastuzumab" | an isotope label (one or two letters-and-digits words, any separator) just before or after a matched name refuses that name |
| M9-B3 | "bevacizumab awwb", "bevacizumab (awwb)" (stored alias `Bevacizumab awwb`) | the suffix-key rule above, and a known suffix as the next word |
| M9-S1 | four lone stored names refused | "Theophylline in dextrose 5% in plastic container" fixed (the item-wide isotope rule is gone; isotopes count only beside a name). Still refused, recorded as known over-refusal: `40-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-rapamycin`, `42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)rapamycin` (the chemical locant reads as an isotope) and `Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate` (an ADC, a different drug). An exact-stored-name exception was not added: 71 stored aliases carry an isotope without a hyphen ("89zr panitumumab", "Lutetium (177lu) dotatate"), and the exception would draw them as the originator. EVEROLIMUS and TRASTUZUMAB VEDOTIN remain reachable by their names and ids. |

Side effect: "osimertinib with [89Zr] trastuzumab" now refuses only trastuzumab (the isotope binds to its neighbour) instead of both. Tests 337.

## Round 10 (audit_evidence_claims/m10) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M10-B1 | a stored alias ending in an isotope ("A33 124i") was accepted | every one- or two-word run inside the matched name is checked for an isotope label |
| M10-B2 | an isotope token stood in for the substance ("18F fluorothymidine" drew FDG) | an isotope-shaped name is accepted only when it is the whole list item ("18f", "566C", "9-ac") |
| M10-B3 | a punctuation-only token used up the two-word budget ("trastuzumab 89 - Zr") | neighbour words are counted after normalisation to letters and digits |
| M10-B4 | respelt qualifiers ("generic-version of", "albumin - bound", "follow-on-biologic") | item guards also see the item with every separator read as one space |

Tests 338.

## Round 11 (audit_evidence_claims/m11) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M11-B1 | punctuation inside the element symbol ("[89Z.r] trastuzumab") | neighbour words are glued as the key glues them (up to the length of a label) and compared with isotope labels; a matched name whose key is an isotope label plus a key of the same drug ("89zr" + "trastuzumab") is refused |
| M11-B2 | a suffix spelt in pieces beyond the five-token window ("bevacizumab a - w w b") | the words after a name are glued the same way and compared with the known biosimilar suffixes |
| M11-S1 | further lone-alias losses (`9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin`, `il-6ra`, `TAK-659F`) | recorded as known over-refusals: their keys end or contain what reads as an isotope label; refusing never draws a wrong drug |

Dose and schedule text after a name is unaffected ("osimertinib 80 mg", "paclitaxel 2 cycles", "cisplatin 75 mg/m2 day 1"). Tests 339.

## Round 12 (audit_evidence_claims/m12) — CHANGES REQUIRED (one blocker)

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M12-B1 | "[89Z.r]-DFO-daratumumab": inside a consumed stored alias the split symbol was checked only as space-joined words, and the chelator kept the remainder from being a same-drug key | every run of the matched name's words is also glued (as the key glues them, up to a label's length) and compared with isotope labels |

A sweep of every unambiguous stored alias run alone (before and after this change) shows no newly refused or changed names. Tests 340.

## Round 13 (audit_evidence_claims/m13) — CHANGES REQUIRED (one blocker)

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M13-B1 | "89ZrDFO-daratumumab": no separator between the isotope and the chelator | a name's key that starts (ends) with a two-letter-element isotope label and whose rest ends (starts) with any key of the same drug is refused, whatever lies between; a neighbouring word with a label fused to its start or end ("DFO89Zr") also refuses, one-letter elements only where case marks the boundary |

Kept narrow on purpose: lower-case chemistry and dose text are not read as isotopes ("13-cis retinoic acid", "paclitaxel 2 cycles", "BKM-120NX"). The stored-alias sweep shows no newly refused names (one formerly refused name, `rad001c`, now resolves). Tests 341.

## Round 14 (audit_evidence_claims/m14) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M14-B1 | "3HVINBLASTINE": a one-letter element in an all-capitals name escaped the case rule (regression of round 13) | in the name's own key a one-letter element counts wherever it is written upper-case |
| M14-B2 | "89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT": a trailing marker kept the drug's key from ending the rest | the same drug's name may sit anywhere after (before) the label |
| M13 residual | "A33124I" | a label closing a stored name of the drug counts when its mass is one used in medicine (I-124); development codes ending in a salt letter ("NSC-118218H", "DX-8951F", "GW572016F") are not labels |

Stored-alias sweep: no newly refused names. Tests 342.

Known data limitation found while fixing, not changed here: ChEMBL lists bevacizumab biosimilar development codes as synonyms of bevacizumab (HLX04, HLX-04, HLX04-O, BAT1706, SB8, MB02, FKB238, BI 695502), so these draw BEVACIZUMAB. Fixing it belongs in the dictionary curation step (resolver and graph), with a re-measurement, not in the map.

## Round 15 (audit_evidence_claims/m15) — CHANGES REQUIRED

Each round found another spelling of an isotope. The rule is now one check on the run-together key, using a table of
radioactive masses used in medicine (H-3, C-11/14, N-13, O-15, F-18, Cu-61..67, Ga-67/68, Zr-89, Y-86/90, Tc-99,
In-111, I-123..131, Lu-177, Ra-223, ...; stable 2H/13C/15N excluded so "13-cis" is chemistry):

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M15-B1 | "89-ZrDFO trastuzumab" | a medical isotope anywhere in the list item (two-letter elements across words; one-letter elements within a word) refuses an unlabelled drug |
| M15-B2 | "3hvinblastine" (lower case) | same rule; the mass table, not the case, decides |
| — | — | a drug whose own preferred name carries a label (LUTETIUM ... LU-177, FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18, RADIUM RA 223) is the labelled product and is not refused |

Same list item, fail closed: "osimertinib with [89Zr] trastuzumab" refuses both; "osimertinib, [89Zr] trastuzumab" keeps
osimertinib. Additional known over-refusals: `GS-11CAL-101`, `3en3hg4wsw` (codes that read as C-11 / H-3). Tests 343.

| M16-S1 | the item's isotope state was recomputed for every match (seconds under the lock) | computed once per item (cached) |

Stored-alias sweep: no change. Tests 344.

### M16-S1: the item's isotope state is now computed once

**FIXED for the exact reported extra work.** At 125 / 1,000 occurrences, instrumentation records **one whole-item scan** and **250 / 2,000 within-word helper calls**, respectively. M16 originally recorded 125 / 1,000 whole-item scans and 15,625 / 1,000,000 within-word calls. M17 recorded one scan with 125 / 1,000 within-word calls. The preceding M18 repair checks both the canonical word and its punctuation piece, which are identical for `isa`; this doubles those within-word calls but does not restore per-match whole-item rescanning. `@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=4096)` caches the pure tuple-keyed computation (`src/query.py:168-198`), called from `_marked` at `src/query.py:423-425`. The same single ISATUXIMAB entity survives. [Fresh instrumentation](cost/repeated_match_instrumentation.json), [reused script](cost/repeated_match_followup.py).

For the legal 3,999-character **answer** field, the fresh HTTP check takes **2.44896 s**, queues EGFR for **2.36627 s**, and takes **2.95730 s** warm. The ordinary map after completion takes 0.04612 s; all return 200. This verifies the repair without claiming all repeated-match work was removed. `_marked` still constructs/scans token lists before checking the cache and runs before entity deduplication (`src/query.py:368-396,581-591`). [HTTP](cost/isolated_repeated_match_cost_http.json), [closed owned server / unchanged DB](cost/repeated_match_cost_lifecycle.json).

`engine.lock` serializes API operations (`src/app.py:247-263`). These 3,999/4,000-character workloads use direct linking or the **answer field**; explore correctly rejects more than 500 characters. Limits precede NFKC (`src/app.py:239-243,260`; `src/query.py:117-118,282-284`). Residual costs are should-fix observations, not the reason for the verdict. **No model calls or model cost were incurred.**

## Specification


Specification source: the user's Round 19 brief and original guarantee 3 / approval bar: “a refused product drawn as the originator” is a blocker. Diff reviewed: `git diff 4bed79c1f170ebe1efa3d452ce4398cce17b2113...HEAD -- src tests`.

New `non-radiolabelled trastuzumab` refusal is a separate should-fix recall regression. No scope-creep finding is identified. Known over-refusals and bevacizumab biosimilar-code curation do not determine these findings.

import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import types

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))

if len(sys.argv) > 1:
    import query
    import textrules as T
    mode, kind, size = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], int(sys.argv[3])
    module = query
    if mode == 'm4':
        module = types.ModuleType('perf_m4_query')
        module.__file__ = str(ROOT / 'src/query.py')
        sys.modules[module.__name__] = module
        code = subprocess.run(['git', 'show', 'bf436c6:src/query.py'], cwd=ROOT,
                              capture_output=True, check=True).stdout.decode('utf-8')
        exec(compile(code, module.__file__, 'exec'), module.__dict__)
    kg = module.OncoKG()
    kg.conn.execute('PRAGMA query_only=ON')
    text = {'plain': ' '.join(['x'] * size),
            'drug': ' '.join(['x'] * size + ['osimertinib']),
            'split': ';'.join(['x'] * size),
            'single': 'x' * size,
            'late_qualifier': ' '.join(['x'] * size + ['biosimilar', 'bevacizumab'])}[kind]
    calls = {'is_distinct_product': 0, 'is_radiotracer': 0, 'characters': 0}
    for name in ['is_distinct_product', 'is_radiotracer']:
        original = getattr(T, name)
        def wrapped(value, name=name, original=original):
            calls[name] += 1
            calls['characters'] += len(value)
            return original(value)
        setattr(T, name, wrapped)
    start = time.perf_counter()
    entities = kg.link_entities(text)
    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start
    kg.conn.close()
    print(json.dumps({'mode': mode, 'kind': kind, 'size': size, 'characters': len(text),
                     'seconds': elapsed, 'calls': calls, 'entities': entities}))
else:
    env = {**os.environ, 'PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE': '1', 'PYTHONUTF8': '1',
           'PYTHONIOENCODING': 'utf-8', 'TEMP': str(OUT / 'tmp'),
           'TMP': str(OUT / 'tmp'), 'TMPDIR': str(OUT / 'tmp')}
    cases = [('plain', n) for n in [50, 100, 200, 250, 500, 1000]]
    cases += [('drug', 244), ('split', 250), ('single', 500), ('late_qualifier', 235)]
    rows = []
    for kind, size in cases:
        for mode in ['m4', 'HEAD']:
            try:
                proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-B', __file__, mode, kind, str(size)],
                                      cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True, timeout=20)
                row = json.loads(proc.stdout) if proc.returncode == 0 else {
                    'mode': mode, 'kind': kind, 'size': size, 'error': proc.stderr.decode('utf-8', 'replace')}
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                row = {'mode': mode, 'kind': kind, 'size': size, 'timeout_seconds': 20}
            rows.append(row)
            (OUT / 'perf_results.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
            print(json.dumps(row), flush=True)

import hashlib
import json
import os

import socket

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent

BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
CASES = ['trastuzumab', 'radio-labelled trastuzumab', 'radio-labeled trastuzumab', 'radiolabelled trastuzumab', 'radiolabeled trastuzumab', 'trastuzumab radioactively labelled', 'trastuzumab radiolabelled with iodine-124', '99m-technetium trastuzumab', 'technetium-99m trastuzumab', 'trastuzumab 99m-technetium', 'trastuzumab technetium-99m', 'osimertinib, radio-labelled trastuzumab', 'radio-labelled trastuzumab, osimertinib', 'fluorine-18 fluorodeoxyglucose', 'lead compound osimertinib', 'BKM120', 'AZD9291', 'osimertinib 80 mg', 'paclitaxel 2 cycles', '13-cis retinoic acid', 'b.i.o.similar bevacizumab', 'lip.osomal doxorubicin']


def post(endpoint, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + "/api/" + endpoint,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    with response:
        return {"endpoint": endpoint, "input": text, "status": response.status,
                "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start,
                "response": json.loads(response.read())}

with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "owned port already occupied"
db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1", "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TEMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TMPDIR":str(OUT/"tmp")}
with (OUT / "independent_m19_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    results = []
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        for name in CASES:
            for endpoint in ("resolve", "explore"):
                results.append(post(endpoint, name))
            if not name.startswith("CHEMBL"):
                results.append(post("ask", f"Which trials evaluate {name}?"))
                results.append(post("ask", f"Who sponsors trials of {name}?"))
        (OUT / "independent_m19_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(results,
            ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps([{"input": r["input"], "status": r["status"],
            "center": r["response"].get("center", {}).get("id"),
            "refused": r["response"].get("refused")}
            for r in results if r["endpoint"] == "explore"], indent=2))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        (OUT / "independent_m19_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "port_closed": closed,
            "database_unchanged": hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == before,
            "requests": len(results), "all_200": all(r["status"] == 200 for r in results),
            "page_matches_worktree": page == (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_bytes()
        }, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

    def counted(key,one_letter=True):
        calls['one_letter_true' if one_letter else 'one_letter_false']+=1
        calls['characters']+=len(key)
        return original(key,one_letter)
    query._medical_isotope_in=counted
    try:
        value=' '.join(['isa']*n)
        start=time.perf_counter();entities=kg.link_entities(value);elapsed=time.perf_counter()-start
        rows.append(dict(matches=n,characters=len(value),helper_calls=calls,seconds_with_instrumentation=elapsed,entities=entities))
    finally:
        query._medical_isotope_in=original;kg.conn.close()

def post(path, payload):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    try:
        res = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        res = e
    return {"status": res.status, "body": json.loads(res.read())}

"""Grounded question answering over the graph.

The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.

Three properties the answering layer holds to:

* **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
  each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
* **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
  the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
  against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
  graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
  directly.
* **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
  reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import functools
import re
import sqlite3
import sys
import unicodedata
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T

DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# evidence
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
@dataclass
class Evidence:
    statement: str
    edge_id: str
    relation: str
    source: str
    source_record: str | None
    fetched_at: str | None
    confidence: float
    tier: str | None = None
    url: str | None = None
    detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)

    def citation(self) -> str:
        base = self.source_record or self.source
        return f"[{base}]"


@dataclass
class Answer:
    question: str
    intent: str
    entities: list[dict]
    text: str
    evidence: list[Evidence]
    generated_by: str
    checks: dict

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "question": self.question,
            "intent": self.intent,
            "entities": self.entities,
            "answer": self.text,
            "generated_by": self.generated_by,
            "checks": self.checks,
            "evidence": [
                {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
                 "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
                 "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
                 "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
                for e in self.evidence
            ],
        }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# engine
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
# them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
# nanoparticles").
_JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
            "or/and", "and/or"}



# every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
_DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
                        if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"


def _plain(text: str) -> str:
    """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
    return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))


def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
    """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
    return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))


def _fold(text: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))



# Mass numbers of the isotopes used in medicine, per element (lower case).
# Radioactive labels only: stable 2H, 13C and 15N mark chemistry ("13-cis"), not tracers.
_MEDICAL_MASS = {"h": {3}, "c": {11, 14}, "n": {13}, "o": {15}, "f": {18},
                 "p": {32}, "s": {35}, "cu": {61, 62, 64, 67}, "ga": {67, 68}, "br": {76, 77},
                 "rb": {82}, "sr": {89, 90}, "y": {86, 90}, "zr": {89}, "tc": {99}, "in": {111},
                 "i": {123, 124, 125, 131}, "sm": {153}, "lu": {177}, "re": {186, 188},
                 "pb": {203, 212}, "at": {211}, "ra": {223}, "ac": {225}}


def _medical_isotope(label: str) -> bool:
    m = re.match(r"^(\d{1,3})m?([a-z]{1,2})$", (label or "").lower())
    return bool(m and int(m.group(1)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(2), set()))


def _medical_isotope_in(key: str, one_letter: bool = True) -> bool:
    """Is a medical isotope written anywhere in this run-together key, mass first ("89zr",
    "a33124i") or a two-letter element first ("lu177")? One-letter elements only when
    `one_letter` (within a word: "3hvinblastine"; across words "13 nsclc" is not N-13)."""
    for m in re.finditer(r"(\d+)m?([a-z]{1,2})", key):
        mass = int(m.group(1)[-3:])
        el = m.group(2)
        if mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el, set()):
            return True
        if one_letter and mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el[:1], set()):
            return True
    for m in re.finditer(r"([a-z]{2})(\d{2,3})(?!\d)", key):
        if int(m.group(2)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(1), set()):
            return True
    return False




# Element names written out, mapped to the symbols the mass table uses.
_ELEMENT_NAMES = {"hydrogen": "h", "tritium": "h", "carbon": "c", "nitrogen": "n", "oxygen": "o",
                  "fluorine": "f", "phosphorus": "p", "sulfur": "s", "sulphur": "s",
                  "copper": "cu", "gallium": "ga", "bromine": "br", "rubidium": "rb",
                  "strontium": "sr", "yttrium": "y", "zirconium": "zr", "technetium": "tc",
                  "indium": "in", "iodine": "i", "samarium": "sm", "lutetium": "lu",
                  "lutecium": "lu", "rhenium": "re", "lead": "pb", "astatine": "at",
                  "radium": "ra", "actinium": "ac"}
# Words that say the substance carries a radioactive label.
_LABEL_WORDS = {"tritiated", "tritium", "radiotracer", "radiotracers", "radioligand", "radionuclide",
                "radioconjugate", "radioimmunoconjugate", "radiopharmaceutical", "radioactive",
                "radiolabelled", "radiolabeled", "radiolabel"}

@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=4096)
def _item_has_isotope(words: tuple) -> bool:
    """Is a medical isotope written anywhere in this list item? Worked out once per item.

    Two-letter elements are read across words ("trastuzumab Ga 68"); a one-letter element
    only inside one word, mass first ("3hvinblastine") or element first ("I-124-labelled"),
    so "13 NSCLC" is not N-13 and "BKM120" is not M-120."""
    if _medical_isotope_in(T.canonical_key(" ".join(words)), one_letter=False):
        return True
    flat = [x.lower() for x in _alnum_words(" ".join(words))]
    # each word, and each neighbouring pair glued ("radio-labelled", "tri tiated")
    joined = flat + [a + b for a, b in zip(flat, flat[1:])]
    if any(x in _LABEL_WORDS or x.startswith(("radiolabel", "radioiod")) for x in joined):
        return True                          # "radiolabelled", "tritiated", "radiotracer"
    # element names written out, in either order ("iodine-124", "99m-technetium")
    for a, b in zip(flat, flat[1:]):
        for name, num in ((a, b), (b, a)):
            el = _ELEMENT_NAMES.get(name)
            m = re.fullmatch(r"(\d{1,3})m?", num)
            if el and m and int(m.group(1)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el, set()):
                return True
    for w in words:
        # the word as written, and its punctuation-separated pieces with each neighbouring
        # pair glued, so a boundary the punctuation marks is kept ("labelled-I-124")
        parts = [p.lower() for p in _alnum_words(w)]
        for key in [T.canonical_key(w)] + parts + [a + b for a, b in zip(parts, parts[1:])]:
            if _medical_isotope_in(key):
                return True
            for m in re.finditer(r"(?<![a-z])([a-z])(\d{1,3})(?!\d)", key):
                if int(m.group(2)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(1), set()):
                    return True
    return False

def _labelled(drug_name: str) -> bool:
    """Does a drug's preferred name carry an isotope (it is the radiolabelled product)?"""
    key = T.canonical_key(drug_name)
    if _medical_isotope_in(key):
        return True
    for m in re.finditer(r"\d+", key):         # element before the mass ("f18", "y90")
        mass = int(m.group()[:3])
        before = key[max(0, m.start() - 2):m.start()]
        if mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(before, set()) or mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(before[-1:], set()):
            return True
    return False

def _isotope_edge(word: str) -> bool:
    """An isotope label fused to the start or the end of one word ("89ZrDFO", "DFO89Zr").

    A one-letter element counts only where the case marks the boundary ("89FDFO"), so
    dose text such as "2cycles" or "5in" is not read as carbon or indium."""
    for i in range(2, min(6, len(word)) + 1):
        for lab, rest in ((word[:i], word[i:]), (word[-i:], word[:-i])):
            # the label must meet letters, not run on into more digits ("89Zr" is whole)
            if not rest or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
                continue
            if not (rest[:1] if lab == word[:i] else rest[-1:]).isalpha():
                continue
            elem = re.sub(r"^[\d\s\-\[\]m]*", "", lab)
            if len(elem) == 2 and elem[0].isupper() and elem[1].islower():
                return True                      # "Zr", "Ga", "Lu"
            if len(elem) == 1 and elem.isupper() and (rest[:1].isupper() if lab == word[:i]
                                                      else rest[-1:].isupper()):
                return True                      # "89FDFO"-like, case-marked
    return False

def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
    """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?

    The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
    biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
    work grows linearly with the item."""
    def hit(text: str) -> bool:
        # the alias key ignores punctuation, underscores and accents, so the guards also
        # see the text with those folded ("bevacizumab-(awwb)", "bevacizumab_awwb",
        # "bevácizumab-awwb")
        plain = _plain(text)
        # and with every separator (punctuation, underscore, spaces) read as one space,
        # so "generic-version of", "albumin - bound" meet the multiword patterns
        return any(T.is_distinct_product(v) or T.is_radiotracer(v)
                   for v in {text, plain, re.sub(r"(?:[^\w\s]|_)+", "-", plain).strip("-"),
                             " ".join(_alnum_words(plain))})

    if hit(" ".join(words)):
        return True
    if any(T.canonical_key(w) in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES for w in words):
        return True
    return any(hit(" ".join(words[i:j])) for i in range(len(words))
               for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1))

class OncoKG:
    def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
                 resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
        self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
        self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
        self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
        self._resolve_cache: dict = {}
        self.disease = resolved["disease"]
        self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
                        if t.get("symbol")}
        # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
        # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
        self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
        for row in self.conn.execute(
                "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
            self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
            self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
        # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
        for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
            if same in self.companies:
                self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
        self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}

    # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
    def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
        # one spelling for every check: compatibility forms folded, every dash a hyphen
        question = _fold(question)
        blocked = self._resolver_blocked(question)
        found = self._link(question)
        whole = self._whole_name(question, blocked, found)
        if whole:
            return [whole]

import concurrent.futures
import hashlib
import json
import time
import urllib.request
import urllib.error
from pathlib import Path

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
BASE = "http://127.0.0.1:8840"
DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"


def request(payload=None, raw=None, path="/api/explore"):
    data = raw if raw is not None else json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=False).encode()
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=data,
                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as err:
        response = err
    content = response.read()
    try:
        body = json.loads(content)
    except ValueError:
        body = content.decode(errors="replace")
    return {"status": response.status, "bytes": len(content),
            "ms": round((time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000, 3), "body": body}


def digest():
    return hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    assert run.returncode == 0

    regression = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(OUT / "regression_http.py")],
        cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONIOENCODING":"utf-8"})
    (OUT / "regression_http_stdout.txt").write_bytes(regression.stdout + regression.stderr)
    assert regression.returncode == 0

    # Load the previous linker/answer module in memory; use the identical database.
    old = types.ModuleType("m2b_old_query")
    old.__file__ = str(ROOT / "src/query.py")
    sys.modules[old.__name__] = old
    code = subprocess.run(["git", "show", "bfcb83d:src/query.py"], cwd=ROOT,
                          capture_output=True, check=True).stdout.decode("utf-8")
    exec(compile(code, old.__file__, "exec"), old.__dict__)
    baseline = old.OncoKG()
    baseline.conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only=ON")
    html = (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    questions = json.loads(re.search(r"const EX2 = (\[.*?\]);", html, re.S).group(1))
    # Verify CLI/dashboard demos are covered by the page list, without running
    # their entrypoints (which write tracked report/dashboard artifacts).
    sources = {}
    for filename, variable in [("src/query.py", "questions"),
                               ("src/make_dashboard_data.py", "demo_questions")]:
        parsed = ast.parse((ROOT / filename).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        assignments = [n for n in ast.walk(parsed) if isinstance(n, ast.Assign)
                       and any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == variable for t in n.targets)]
        value = assignments[-1].value
        if isinstance(value, ast.BoolOp):
            value = value.values[-1]
        sources[filename] = ast.literal_eval(value)
        assert set(sources[filename]) <= set(questions)
    answers = []
    for question in questions:
        previous = baseline.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
        current = post("/api/ask", {"text": question})
        answers.append({"question": question, "http_status": current["status"],
                        "identical_to_bfcb83d": current["body"] == previous,
                        "current": current["body"], "previous": previous})
    baseline.conn.close()
    (OUT / "http_demo_results.json").write_text(json.dumps({"source_lists": sources,
        "answers": answers}, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps({"demo_questions": len(answers),
        "all_http_200": all(a["http_status"] == 200 for a in answers),
        "all_identical_to_bfcb83d": all(a["identical_to_bfcb83d"] for a in answers)}))
finally:
    proc.terminate()
    proc.wait(timeout=10)
    log.close()
    with socket.socket() as s:
        port_closed = s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
    after = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    (OUT / "http_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({"pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT,
        "server_exit": proc.returncode, "port_closed": port_closed,
        "database_unchanged": after == before, "db_sha256_before": before,
        "db_sha256_after": after}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

def post(endpoint, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + "/api/" + endpoint,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    with response:
        return {"endpoint": endpoint, "input": text, "status": response.status,
                "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start,
                "response": json.loads(response.read())}

with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "owned port already occupied"
db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
env = {**os.environ, "TMP": str(OUT), "TEMP": str(OUT), "TMPDIR": str(OUT), "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"}
with (OUT / "new_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    results = []
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        for name in CASES:
            for endpoint in ("resolve", "explore"):
                results.append(post(endpoint, name))
            if not name.startswith("CHEMBL"):
                results.append(post("ask", f"Which trials evaluate {name}?"))
                results.append(post("ask", f"Who sponsors trials of {name}?"))
        (OUT / "new_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(results,
            ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps([{"input": r["input"], "status": r["status"],
            "center": r["response"].get("center", {}).get("id"),
            "refused": r["response"].get("refused")}
            for r in results if r["endpoint"] == "explore"], indent=2))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        (OUT / "new_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "port_closed": closed,
            "database_unchanged": hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == before,

"""Verify repository byte identity, report references, and owned-service cleanup."""
from pathlib import Path
import datetime,hashlib,json,re,socket,subprocess
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent;ROOT=OUT.parents[1]
before=json.loads((OUT/'integrity_before.json').read_text(encoding='utf8'))
def git(*args):
    p=subprocess.run(['git',*args],cwd=ROOT,capture_output=True)
    return dict(code=p.returncode,stdout=p.stdout.decode('utf8','replace'),stderr=p.stderr.decode('utf8','replace'))
changed=[];missing=[]
for rel,digest in before['tracked_sha256'].items():
    p=ROOT/rel
    if not p.exists():missing.append(rel)
    elif hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()!=digest:changed.append(rel)
tracked=set(git('ls-files','-z')['stdout'].split('\0'))-{''}
ports={}
owned_ports=set()
for lifecycle_path in OUT.rglob('*lifecycle.json'):
    lifecycle=json.loads(lifecycle_path.read_text(encoding='utf8'))
    if isinstance(lifecycle,dict) and isinstance(lifecycle.get('port'),int):
        owned_ports.add(lifecycle['port'])
for port in sorted(owned_ports):
    with socket.socket() as sock:
        sock.settimeout(.2);ports[str(port)]=sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1',port))!=0
report=(OUT/'REPORT.md').read_text(encoding='utf8')
links=re.findall(r'\]\(([^)]+)\)',report)
local=[s for s in links if not re.match(r'https?://',s)]
broken=[s for s in local if s!='integrity_final.json' and not (OUT/s.split('#',1)[0]).exists()]
db=hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest()
status=git('status','--short')
result=dict(time=datetime.datetime.now().astimezone().isoformat(),head_before=before['head'],
    head_after=git('rev-parse','HEAD')['stdout'].strip(),tracked_files_hashed=len(before['tracked_sha256']),
    changed_tracked_files=changed,missing_tracked_files=missing,new_tracked_paths=sorted(tracked-set(before['tracked_sha256'])),
    tracked_diff=git('diff','--exit-code'),staged_diff=git('diff','--cached','--exit-code'),
    git_status=status,initial_status=before['status'],database_sha256_before=before['db_sha256'],
    database_sha256_after=db,database_unchanged=db==before['db_sha256'],owned_ports_closed=ports,
    local_report_links_checked=len(local),external_citations=len(links)-len(local),broken_report_links=broken,
    report_final_line=report.rstrip().splitlines()[-1],report_sha256=hashlib.sha256((OUT/'REPORT.md').read_bytes()).hexdigest())
result['passed']=(not changed and not missing and not result['new_tracked_paths']
    and result['head_before']==result['head_after'] and result['tracked_diff']['code']==result['staged_diff']['code']==0
    and result['database_unchanged'] and all(ports.values()) and not broken and status['stdout']==before['status']
    and result['report_final_line'] in ('VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED','VERDICT: APPROVE'))
(OUT/'integrity_final.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({k:v for k,v in result.items() if k not in ('git_status','initial_status','tracked_diff','staged_diff')},ensure_ascii=False,indent=2))
assert result['passed'],'Final integrity check failed'

"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
click to move on),
then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
        """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.

        `text` is a node id or a name the question linker reads as one entity. A name
        the linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is reported, never
        mapped to the originator. Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the
        page states: trials by start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph,
        sponsors by trials they lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship
        keeps its role (lead sponsor or collaborator)."""
        conn = self.kg.conn
        also: list = []
        matched = None
        row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                           (text,)).fetchone()
        if row:
            nid = row["id"]
        else:
            ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
            refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
            hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
            if not hits:
                if refused:
                    return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
                return {"not_found": text}
            nid, matched = hits[0]["id"], hits[0].get("matched_alias") or hits[0].get("matched_text")
            also = [{"id": e["id"], "type": e["type"], "label": e["label"]} for e in hits[1:]]
            for oid in hits[0].get("also_matching_ids") or []:   # one alias, several ids
                lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                   (oid,)).fetchone()
                if lab and oid != nid:
                    also.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
            # the same words may also name an organisation or a target the linker
            # passed over because a drug alias came first ("Roche" is a folic acid alias)
            key = T.canonical_key(text)
            for cid in self.kg.companies.get(key, []):
                if cid != nid and all(x.get("id") != cid for x in also):
                    lab = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                       (cid,)).fetchone()
                    if lab:
                        also.append({"id": cid, "type": "Company", "label": lab["label"]})
            t = self.kg.targets.get(text.strip().lower())
            if t and t["target_id"] != nid:
                also.append({"id": t["target_id"], "type": "Target", "label": t["symbol"]})
            also += [{"refused": e["label"]} for e in refused]
        node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
                            "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
        if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
            return {"not_found": text}
        rows = conn.execute(
            "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "

def links(text,prefix):
    def replace(m):
        target=m.group(1)
        if re.match(r'https?://|#',target): return m.group(0)
        return ']('+posixpath.normpath(prefix+'/'+target)+')'
    return re.sub(r'\]\(([^)]+)\)',replace,text)
